  [G10.2] Communication preferences and formats
  Potential Concern — severity Moderate — citations partly verified
    Report:   The suitability report contains no explicit statement of the client's communication preferences or formats. The report is silent on whether any communication preferences, accessible formats, language requirements, or literacy considerations were recorded or applied.
    File:     The case file contains multiple references to communication preferences and contact methods. [P9] records 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference' in the Fact Find. [P24] describes Aviva's general policy that clients may specify preferred communication methods (email, written, or other) and that 'Where We are unable to contact You through Your preferred method of communication, We may try to communicate with You by another means of contact, depending on the information You or Your Adviser have given Us.' [P1], [P3], and [P5] set out the Client Agreement framework which references communication via post, phone, email, or 'in any other way a member feels is appropriate.' [P9] also notes that 'Copies of the proposal forms and other documentation may also be held. These may contain sensitive personal data as defined by legislation e.g. health details' and confirms explicit consent to process sensitive personal data.
    Discrepancies:
      - The Fact Find [P9] records a communication preference ('No Preference'), but the suitability report makes no reference to this recorded preference or acknowledges what 'No Preference' means in practical terms for how advice and ongoing communications will be delivered.
      - The suitability report does not state whether the client has any accessibility requirements, literacy considerations, or language needs, despite the Fact Find process [P9] and Client Agreement [P1] contemplating such scenarios.
      - The report does not confirm whether the client's consent to process sensitive personal data [P9] was obtained or acknowledged as part of the advice process, despite health and other sensitive details potentially being held.
    The comparison can be made because the Fact Find [P9] explicitly records communication preference data, and the case file contains the Client Agreement framework [P1], [P3], [P5], [P24] describing how communication preferences are managed. However, a material gap exists between what was captured during fact-finding and what the suitability report itself reflects. The Fact Find records 'No Preference' but the suitability report is entirely silent on this—it does not explain what 'No Preference' means, whether a default method was agreed, or whether any specific considerations apply to this client's communication. The standard contact preference recorded in [P9] is not in itself a vulnerability indicator; the guard acknowledges that 'A standard contact preference is not a vulnerability adjustment; record it, but do not treat its presence as evidence that vulnerability was assessed.' This means the presence of 'No Preference' is not evidence that communication needs were assessed for vulnerability or accessibility. The report's silence on communication preferences, combined with the absence of any recorded communication adjustments or accessibility considerations in the suitability narrative, creates a process risk: the adviser has gathered communication preference data during fact-finding but has not carried that information through into the suitability report or confirmed how it will be applied during ongoing service delivery. This is particularly significant given the Client Agreement [P5] and [P17] confirm an ongoing review and advice service is to be provided 'at least annually'—yet the suitability report does not specify how the client will be contacted or whether any communication barriers have been identified.

    Report:   The suitability report contains no extracted assertions at the canonical model paths `/vulnerability/perClient[]/adjustmentsMade[]` or `/vulnerability/perClient[]/characteristics[]`.     File:     The case file contains: (1) Fact Find recording 'Preferred delivery method: No Preference' [P2] and marketing consent to email [P16]; (2) Risk profile report with sections for 'Investor Experience' and 'Capacity for loss questionnaire', both noting 'No additional notes were recorded' [P19, P23]; (3) Capacity for loss narrative in both suitability report [P5] and file notes [P11] stating the same text: client aware investment values rise and fall, experience limited to workplace pension, underperformance would not impact day-to-day living, with income breakdown of State Pension £230/week, HGV driver earnings £300/week net, Sea Cadets £20/week, spouse Kim contributing £1,100/month [P5, P11]; (4) File notes recording ATR assessment discussion but stating 'No additional notes were recorded' in the risk profile capacity section [P19, P23]; (5) Client Agreement [P17] setting out ongoing review service; (6) Fact Find dated 19/09/2025 [P2] with declaration signed.       - The report recommends Power of Attorney setup [P4] but does not specify whether this is a general prudence suggestion or a response to identified capacity, comprehension, or support need factors.

    The requirement asks whether information in the suitability report relating to vulnerabilities, support needs, communication preferences, capacity, comprehension requirements, and duty of care considerations is fully supported by evidence.   [G10.4] Vulnerability has a material impact on the recommendation (overlay)
  Potential Concern — severity High
    Report:   The suitability report contains no explicit statement that vulnerability has been assessed or that it materially impacts the recommendation. The canonical model holds no value at paths /vulnerability/perClient[]/impactOnRecommendation[] or /vulnerability/foreseeableHarms[]. The report does acknowledge 'capacity for loss' in general terms (P14: 'You are aware that investment values can rise as well as fall...you confirmed that any potential underperformance would not have a detrimental impact on your day-to-day living'). The report notes limited investment experience ('although your experience is limited to your workplace pension arrangements') but does not frame this as a vulnerability requiring material adjustments to suitability. The report recommends a Risk Rating 5 (Balanced) despite the client's own questionnaire score of 4 (Cautious Balanced), and no specific vulnerability overlay is applied to this upgrade.
    File:     The case file contains evidence of potential vulnerability indicators. P1 shows a Capacity for Loss questionnaire where the client answered 'No understanding / knowledge' to three critical questions: understanding of stocks and shares, understanding of Enterprise Investment Schemes and Venture Capital Trusts, and understanding of pension planning and income in retirement. P2 records 'No additional notes were recorded' following discussion of investor experience and capacity for loss despite these concerning responses. P14 (the report itself) states the client has 'limited' investment experience beyond workplace pensions. P3 describes Risk Rating 5 suitability criteria: 'If you have some experience of investments and a degree of understanding of financial matters then you may be suited to a Balanced approach'. The risk profile assessment in P10 and P11 records 'No additional notes were recorded' for agreed risk level justification. The Fact Find (P6, P12) shows current monthly disposable income of £-288.00 (a deficit), yet the recommendation proceeds without addressing this cash flow constraint as a vulnerability factor. The planning documents (P5, P8, P9, P16) model the client reaching age 100 with projected deficits in later years (P8 shows negative surplus/deficits from age 100 onwards). P20 mentions 'Power of attorney' as a prudent step but does not connect this to identified vulnerability or lack of capacity requiring such arrangements.
    Discrepancies:
      - The report recommends Risk Rating 5 (Balanced) despite the client scoring 4 (Cautious Balanced) on the attitude to risk questionnaire and demonstrating 'No understanding / knowledge' of stocks and shares, pension planning, and EIS/VCTs in the capacity assessment.
      - The report asserts limited impact of underperformance on day-to-day living but the Fact Find shows a monthly cash flow deficit of £-288.00, contradicting the assertion that capacity for loss is unaffected.
      - The file records 'No additional notes' following capacity for loss discussion (P2, P11) despite objective evidence of limited knowledge and negative cash flow, suggesting no meaningful vulnerability assessment occurred.
      - The report does not reflect or explain why the Risk Rating was upgraded from 4 to 5 in terms of any protective measures, communication adjustments, or suitability safeguards appropriate for a client with demonstrably limited knowledge.
      - The recommendation to consolidate multiple pensions and proceed with a balanced growth strategy is not reconciled with the client's stated lack of understanding of pension planning and income in retirement.
    The report asserts general capacity for loss ('any potential underperformance would not have a detrimental impact on day-to-day living') but this assertion is contradicted by the Fact Find data showing current monthly disposable income of -£288.00. The client's own risk questionnaire responses (P1) objectively demonstrate 'No understanding / knowledge' of three critical areas: stocks and shares, pension products, and EIS/VCTs. The report acknowledges 'limited' experience but does not treat this as a material vulnerability affecting suitability. The upgrade of risk rating from 4 to 5 is recorded but not justified in terms of why a client with demonstrably limited understanding should be suitable for a Balanced (rather than Cautious Balanced) approach. FCA guidance FG21/1 emphasizes that low capability combined with limited experience is a vulnerability indicator requiring adjustments to communication, support, and appropriateness of recommendations. The file shows no evidence that such adjustments were applied: no mention of communication in accessible formats, no evidence of enhanced explanation of risks, no documented discussion addressing the knowledge gaps. The record of 'No additional notes' on capacity for loss (P2, P11) despite objective evidence of concern suggests the vulnerability assessment, if conducted, was not documented. P20 mentions Power of Attorney as prudent but does not connect this to any identified incapacity or vulnerability of the client. The cash flow analysis showing monthly deficits, combined with the client's documented lack of understanding of pension income planning, represents a material vulnerability that should drive either rejection of the recommendation or substantial protective adjustments; neither is evident in the report.
